Day of Week
MAP TSM Shell → AI-103 Exam Concept Mapping YOU ALREADY KNOW THIS · LEARN THE MICROSOFT VOCABULARY
Strategic Advantage
AI-103 scenario questions ask: "given this enterprise requirement, choose the right Azure AI service or Foundry architecture." That's exactly what TSM Shell does across 7 verticals. You're learning Azure vocabulary for patterns you've already built — not learning from scratch. Focus on when to use Foundry Agent Service vs standalone Azure OpenAI vs Azure AI Services.
AI-103 Exam Concept Your TSM Shell Proof Presales Angle Difficulty
Microsoft Foundry Hub + Project architecture TSM Shell's server.js + vertical route modules mirrors the Foundry Hub (shared infra) → Project (isolated config) pattern exactly. "Our platform uses the same hub-project isolation pattern as Azure Foundry — shared infrastructure, isolated agent configs per vertical." EASY
Foundry Agent Service — agents, threads, runs, tools TSM War Room → Strategist chain = agent sessions with context threads. Each "run" is a Groq API call with structured output. "I build production agent pipelines. Foundry Agent Service wraps the same primitives — agents, threads, runs — I already implement manually." STUDY
RAG / Azure AI Search grounding TSM Memory Engine (cross-upload insight + lookupField) = RAG grounding. Document classification pipeline = Azure Document Intelligence. "Our memory engine implements RAG grounding without Azure — same retrieval-before-generate pattern, different infrastructure." EASY
Multi-agent orchestration via Microsoft Agent Framework 7-vertical relay chain: War Room → Strategist → Exec Portal → Explainability = 4-stage multi-agent orchestration with A2A handoffs. "I architected a 4-stage multi-agent chain. Microsoft Agent Framework orchestrates the same pattern at scale with Entra Agent ID identity." EASY
HITL + Responsible AI governance HITL modal in all 7 Executive Portals — named approval, timestamp, audit trail. Explainability drawer on every Strategist page. "Every AI recommendation in TSM requires human sign-off before execution — AI-103 Responsible AI principles are already our production standard." EASY
Content Safety / Prompt Shields / Content Filtering TSM uses structured output validation + HITL gate as a safety layer. Azure Content Safety API adds programmatic filtering. Study the configuration API. "I implement safety gates at the output layer. Azure Content Safety adds jailbreak detection and protected material detection at the API layer." STUDY
Azure Document Intelligence / Content Understanding TSM's extractFile → classifyExtraction → routeDocument pipeline is your proof of document intelligence. Azure wraps this in prebuilt + custom models. "I built a multi-stage document classification pipeline from scratch. Azure Document Intelligence is the enterprise-managed version." STUDY
Foundry Evaluations (AI-assisted + human review) BNCA collective signal layer compares outputs across verticals. Foundry Evaluations does this with built-in quality + safety metrics. "Our platform runs cross-vertical signal aggregation — Foundry Evaluations formalizes this with groundedness, coherence, and safety scores." LEARN
azure-ai-projects SDK / Python API patterns TSM uses vanilla JS + server-side Node.js relay. AI-103 expects Python SDK fluency. This is the biggest technical gap to close. Study azure-ai-projects and azure-ai-inference Python packages. Know how to create agents, add tools, start threads and runs programmatically. LEARN
Azure AI Search — hybrid + semantic ranker TSM Memory Engine uses in-memory lookup. Azure AI Search = managed vector + keyword + semantic hybrid retrieval. Know index creation and query types. "Our memory architecture works like Azure AI Search's hybrid retrieval — combining semantic meaning with exact-match for highest accuracy." STUDY
WEEK 1 AI-103 — Plan & Manage Azure AI + Foundry Foundation JUN 27 – JUL 10 60-90 MIN/DAY · EXAM DAY 21
Week 1 Mission
AI-103 is a developer-level exam (100 min, ~50 questions, $165). It covers 5 domains: Plan & Manage (25-30%), Generative AI & Agents (30-35%), Computer Vision (10-15%), Text Analysis (10-15%), Information Extraction (10-15%). Week 1 + 2 = foundation. Week 3 = simulate and pass. Python SDK fluency and Microsoft Foundry hands-on time are the biggest differentiators.
FRI · DAY 1
ORIENTATION + BOOK
  • Read official AI-103 skills outline: learn.microsoft.com/credentials/certifications/resources/study-guides/ai-103
  • Take Microsoft free practice assessment — baseline your scores by domain
  • Note domains below 70% — these become your Week 3 priority
  • Book AI-103 for Day 21 (Jul 18) at Pearson VUE RIGHT NOW
⏱ 60 min · $165 exam booked today
SAT · DAY 2
FOUNDRY ARCHITECTURE
  • Microsoft Learn: AI-103T00 course — Foundry Hub + Project model
  • Understand: Hub = shared infra (storage, key vault, connections)
  • Understand: Project = isolated workspace per use case
  • Map to TSM Shell: server.js (hub) + vertical routes (projects)
⏱ 75 min · Microsoft Learn AI-103T00
SUN · DAY 3
PLAN & MANAGE DOMAIN
  • Design AI solution architecture: custom app vs Copilot extension vs Foundry template
  • Resource sizing, cost estimation, private endpoints, network security
  • Responsible AI: content filtering, jailbreak detection, Prompt Shields
  • Monitoring: Azure Monitor, Application Insights, Foundry Trace
⏱ 75 min · Plan domain (25-30% of exam)
MON · DAY 4
AZURE AI SERVICES MAP
  • Language service: NER, sentiment, key phrase, summarization, QA
  • Speech: STT, TTS, custom speech, real-time transcription
  • Vision: Image Analysis 4.0, OCR (Read API), Face, Custom Vision
  • CRITICAL: know WHEN to use each service for a given scenario
⏱ 75 min · Service selection patterns
TUE · DAY 5
AZURE OPENAI + PROMPT ENG
  • Azure OpenAI: deployments, model selection (GPT-4o vs o-series), API endpoints
  • System messages, few-shot examples, structured outputs, function calling
  • When to fine-tune vs RAG vs prompt engineering — exam favorite
  • Prompt Shields + content filter severity levels (low/medium/high)
⏱ 75 min · Azure OpenAI deep dive
WED · DAY 6
PYTHON SDK FLUENCY
  • Install azure-ai-projects + azure-ai-inference Python packages
  • Create a Foundry project client: AIProjectClient(endpoint, credential)
  • Deploy a model, call an inference endpoint, read the response
  • This is the biggest gap vs your JS/Node background — code today
⏱ 90 min · Python SDK hands-on lab
THU · DAY 7
PRACTICE QUIZ 1
  • TutorialsDojo: AI-103 free sampler — 20 questions timed mode
  • OpenExamPrep: 25 free AI-103 questions — review all explanations
  • Log weak domains — build your personal gap list
  • Target: understand every wrong answer before moving to Week 2
⏱ 75 min · tutorialsdojo.com + openexamprep.com

WEEK 2 AI-103 — Generative AI + Agents + RAG · Core Developer Domains JUL 4 – JUL 11 75–90 MIN/DAY · HEAVIEST STUDY WEEK
Week 2 Mission
AI-103's heaviest domain is Generative AI & Agentic Solutions (30-35%). This is your sweet spot — you built multi-agent systems. Weeks 2 focuses on Foundry Agent Service primitives, RAG architecture, multi-agent orchestration via Microsoft Agent Framework, and the Python SDK patterns the exam tests with code-completion questions. Use TSM Shell as your mental model — every concept maps to something you already built.
FRI · DAY 8
FOUNDRY AGENT SERVICE
  • Foundry Agent Service primitives: agent definitions, threads, runs, messages
  • Tools: file search, code interpreter, Azure AI Search, web grounding, OpenAPI
  • Python: create agent → create thread → add message → create run → poll run → read output
  • TSM anchor: your Strategist pages = agent sessions with threaded context
⏱ 90 min · Foundry Agent Service lab
SAT · DAY 9
RAG + AZURE AI SEARCH
  • Azure AI Search: index creation, chunking strategies, embedding models
  • Hybrid search: keyword + vector + semantic ranker — know when each wins
  • RAG pattern: retrieve → ground → generate with citations
  • TSM anchor: Memory Engine cross-upload insight = RAG without the managed index
⏱ 90 min · RAG architecture deep dive
SUN · DAY 10
MULTI-AGENT ORCHESTRATION
  • Microsoft Agent Framework: orchestrator → specialist agent pattern
  • MCP (Model Context Protocol) — tool registration and invocation
  • A2A (Agent2Agent) protocol — cross-agent context handoff
  • Entra Agent ID: managed identity for agent-to-resource authentication
⏱ 90 min · Agent orchestration patterns
MON · DAY 11
DOCUMENT INTELLIGENCE + VISION
  • Document Intelligence: prebuilt models (invoice, receipt, ID) vs custom models
  • Content Understanding: OCR + layout analysis + field extraction for RAG grounding
  • Image Analysis 4.0: captioning, tagging, object detection, dense captions
  • CRITICAL trap: know when Document Intelligence vs Content Understanding vs OCR Read
⏱ 75 min · Vision + Document domain (10-15% each)
TUE · DAY 12
TEXT + SPEECH + TRANSLATION
  • Language service: NER, PII detection, summarization, classification, QA
  • Speech: real-time STT, batch transcription, custom speech, TTS neural voices
  • Azure Translator vs LLM translation — when each is appropriate
  • Hardest day: service-selection scenarios — practice with OpenExamPrep
⏱ 90 min · Text + Speech domain (10-15% each)
WED · DAY 13
FOUNDRY EVALUATIONS + SAFETY
  • Foundry Evaluations: groundedness, coherence, relevance, safety metrics
  • AI-assisted evaluators vs human review — when each is required
  • Content Safety API: categories, severity levels (0-6), blocklist configs
  • Foundry Trace: distributed tracing for agent observability — know the SDK calls
⏱ 75 min · Evaluations + Safety domain
THU · DAY 14
PRACTICE TEST 1 + STORIES
  • Full timed practice exam — TutorialsDojo or MindMesh · 100 min
  • Score by domain — flag any below 65% for Week 3 drilling
  • Target: overall ≥ 65% this first attempt
  • After exam: narrate 2 TSM Shell stories in Azure vocabulary
⏱ 120 min · Practice exam + story work

WEEK 3 AI-103 — Scenario Drilling · Code Patterns · Exam Day JUL 12 – JUL 18 60–90 MIN/DAY · EXAM DAY 21
Week 3 Mission
No new concepts in Week 3. Drilling, scoring, code pattern review, and story-building only. By Day 18 score ≥ 75% consistently on full practice exams. Day 21 = AI-103 exam day. Key traps to avoid: confusing Document Intelligence vs Content Understanding, choosing fine-tuning when RAG is correct, missing Entra Agent ID as the auth answer for agent-to-resource calls.

  • Key exam traps: Document Intelligence vs Content Understanding vs OCR Read API
  • Key exam traps: Fine-tuning vs RAG — know the 3 scenarios where fine-tuning wins
  • Key exam traps: Custom Vision vs Image Analysis 4.0 — rarely use Custom Vision in 2026
⏱ 75 min · Exam trap drilling

PRESALES Interview Story Bank — TSM Shell → AB-100 Domain Mapped USE THESE IN EVERY SALESFORCE / MICROSOFT / SERVICENOW INTERVIEW
How to Use This
Every presales interview has 5 question types. Each one maps to an AB-100 domain. Your answer is always: TSM Shell story → Microsoft vocabulary → ROI. Hover each question to see the answer framework.
"Tell me about a time you architected a multi-agent AI solution for an enterprise problem." DESIGN DOMAIN
→ "I architected a 4-stage agentic AI chain for supply chain crisis management — Understand (Situation Room) → Decide (Strategist V2 with 3 parallel scenario models) → Execute (Executive Portal with live tracking) → Trust (Explainability layer with confidence scoring and reasoning chains). Each stage is a discrete agent passing structured context to the next via a relay bus — the same A2A pattern Microsoft Foundry uses. The chain reduced decision time from days to under 30 minutes for an $8.2M exposure scenario."
"How do you ensure AI recommendations are trustworthy and auditable for enterprise clients?" RESPONSIBLE AI
→ "We built an explainability contract into every vertical — every AI recommendation surfaces a confidence score, evidence sources, reasoning chain, and impact delta before a human sees it. Nothing executes without explicit HITL approval in the Executive Portal — named, timestamped, auditable. That's Microsoft's Responsible AI Standard applied in production: transparency, accountability, and human oversight baked into the architecture, not bolted on."
"Walk me through how you'd assess whether agentic AI is the right solution for a client's problem." PLAN DOMAIN
→ "I start with three questions: Is the decision repeatable enough to encode? Does it require context from multiple sources? And is there a human in the loop who can validate before action? If yes to all three, agentic AI fits. I then quantify the ROI: in healthcare denial management, that's $48K in stalled revenue recoverable in 90 days. In supply chain, it's a 6-hour decision window on $8.2M exposure. The ROI model goes into the client brief before we design anything."
"How have you deployed AI governance and compliance in a production environment?" DEPLOY DOMAIN
→ "Our platform ships with governance as architecture: HITL approval at every execution stage, session-token auth via HttpOnly cookies, HMAC-signed tokens, client-isolated data stores, and a full explainability audit trail per decision. We also built a Collective Intelligence layer that aggregates signals across all 7 verticals in real time — so leadership sees platform-wide risk posture, not just vertical-specific alerts. That's the monitoring and telemetry layer Microsoft requires for responsible agentic deployment."
"What's your process for translating a client's business problem into an AI solution design?" DESIGN + PRESALES
→ "I run a 3-step discovery: first, identify the decision bottleneck — where does a human spend time analyzing before acting? Second, map the data sources that inform that decision. Third, design the agent chain: intake → classify → analyze → recommend → approve → execute. I've done this across 7 verticals — the pattern is the same, the domain knowledge changes. I can demo a live version of this process in any of those verticals in under 20 minutes."

FLASHCARDS Interactive Study Cards — AI-103 + AB-100
What is Model Context Protocol (MCP)?
[ TAP TO FLIP ]
AB-100 · DEPLOY DOMAIN
An open standard protocol that allows AI models to access external tools, data sources, and services in a standardized way. TSM Shell uses MCP for cross-agent tool integration.
